GITHUB DEPENDABOT :
# .github/dependabot.yml
version: 2
updates:
  - package-ecosystem: "npm"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10
    reviewers:
      - "developer-team"

SCAN PRE-COMMIT :
# .husky/pre-commit
#!/bin/sh
npm audit --audit-level=high
if [ $? -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "❌ Vulnerabilités détectées ! Fix avant commit."
  exit 1
fi

RÈGLE ADDITIONNELLE : Lock versions
# package-lock.json
# Commit ce fichier pour garantir versions exactes
# Empêche supply chain attacks (dependency confusion)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
9. CONFIGURATION SÉCURISÉE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

RÈGLE : Defaults sécurisés, hardening en production

✅ BON EXEMPLE :
// Production config
const helmet = require('helmet');

app.use(helmet({
  contentSecurityPolicy: {
    directives: {
      defaultSrc: ["'self'"],
      scriptSrc: ["'self'"],
      styleSrc: ["'self'", "'unsafe-inline'"],
    }
  },
  hsts: {
    maxAge: 31536000,
    includeSubDomains: true,
    preload: true,
  },
  frameguard: { action: 'deny' },
  noSniff: true,
  xssFilter: true,
}));

app.disable('x-powered-by'); // Masquer Express

// Cookie sécurisé
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true,
  secure: true, // HTTPS only
  sameSite: 'strict',
  maxAge: 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000, // 24h
});

❌ MAUVAIS EXEMPLE :
// Pas de helmet
// Cookie non sécurisé
res.cookie('session', token);
// Accessible via JavaScript (XSS), HTTP (MITM)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
10. CRYPTOGRAPHIE CORRECTE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

RÈGLE : Utiliser librairies éprouvées, JAMAIS "roll your own crypto"

✅ BON EXEMPLE - Hashing passwords :
const bcrypt = require('bcryptjs');
const hash = await bcrypt.hash(password, 10);

✅ BON EXEMPLE - Encryption :
const crypto = require('crypto');
const algorithm = 'aes-256-gcm';
const key = crypto.scryptSync(password, 'salt', 32);
const iv = crypto.randomBytes(16);
const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv(algorithm, key, iv);
// ... (voir S3.2 pour code complet)

❌ MAUVAIS EXEMPLE :
// JAMAIS faire ça !
const hash = email + password; // Pas de hashing
const hash = md5(password); // MD5 cassé depuis longtemps
const hash = sha1(password); // SHA1 aussi cassé
const encrypted = btoa(text); // base64 n'est PAS du chiffrement !

RÈGLE ADDITIONNELLE : Random sécurisé
// ✅ BON
const crypto = require('crypto');
const token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex');

// ❌ MAUVAIS
const token = Math.random().toString(36); // Pas cryptographiquement sécurisé
S4.2 Code Review Checklist
CHECKLIST REVUE DE CODE SÉCURISÉ

Avant de merger TOUTE Pull Request, vérifier :

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ AUTHENTIFICATION & AUTORISATION                             │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Tous endpoints protégés avec middleware authenticate      │
│ □ Vérification ownership pour ressources privées            │
│ □ Pas de données sensibles dans JWT payload                 │
│ □ Tokens expiration définie                                 │
│ □ Password hashing avec bcrypt (min 10 rounds)              │
│ □ Rate limiting sur endpoints sensibles                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ INPUT VALIDATION                                            │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Validation côté serveur (express-validator)               │
│ □ Sanitization user inputs (escape HTML)                    │
│ □ Whitelist validation (allowed values)                     │
│ □ Type checking (string, number, email, etc.)               │
│ □ Length limits (min/max)                                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DATABASE                                                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Parameterized queries (pas de string concatenation)       │
│ □ Supabase client ou prepared statements                    │
│ □ Pas de SELECT * (sélectionner colonnes explicites)        │
│ □ Index sur colonnes fréquemment requêtées                  │
│ □ Transactions pour opérations multi-steps                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ OUTPUT ENCODING                                             │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ React escape automatique utilisé (pas dangerouslySetInnerHTML)│
│ □ CSP headers configurés                                    │
│ □ Pas de eval() ou Function() avec user input               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ SECRETS & CREDENTIALS                                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Pas de secrets hardcodés                                  │
│ □ Utilisation process.env.XXX                               │
│ □ .env dans .gitignore                                      │
│ □ Tokens Facebook chiffrés en DB                            │
│ □ API keys dans Railway secrets                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ERROR HANDLING                                              │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Try-catch autour code async                               │
│ □ Messages génériques en production                         │
│ □ Pas de stack traces exposées                              │
│ □ Logging structuré des erreurs                             │
│ □ Sentry capture exceptions                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ LOGGING                                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Pas de passwords loggés                                   │
│ □ Pas de tokens JWT complets loggés                         │
│ □ Events sécurité loggés (login, access, changes)           │
│ □ Structured logging (JSON)                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DEPENDENCIES                                                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ npm audit sans vulnérabilités high/critical               │
│ □ Dependencies version lockées (package-lock.json)          │
│ □ Pas de dépendances obsolètes (> 2 ans)                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ CONFIGURATION                                               │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Helmet middleware activé                                  │
│ □ CORS configuré strictement                                │
│ □ HTTPS forcé en production                                 │
│ □ Cookies avec flags Secure + HttpOnly                      │
│ □ x-powered-by header désactivé                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TESTS                                                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ □ Tests unitaires pour logique critique                     │
│ □ Tests validation input (cas normaux + edge cases)         │
│ □ Tests autorisation (user A ne voit pas data user B)       │
│ □ Tests rate limiting                                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

EXEMPLE WORKFLOW GITHUB :

.github/workflows/security-check.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
name: Security Checks

on: [pull_request]

jobs:
  security:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci
      
      - name: Audit dependencies
        run: npm audit --audit-level=high
      
      - name: Run tests
        run: npm test
      
      - name: Lint code
        run: npm run lint
      
      - name: Check for secrets
        uses: trufflesecurity/trufflehog@main
        with:
          path: ./
      
      - name: SAST scan
        uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@v2
S4.3 Pair Programming & Security
PAIR PROGRAMMING POUR SÉCURITÉ

Technique : 2 développeurs travaillent ensemble sur code sensible

QUAND UTILISER :
- Authentification & autorisation
- Paiements
- Encryption
- Intégrations API critiques (Facebook, OpenAI)
- Gestion des secrets

RÔLES :
Driver : Écrit le code
Navigator : Review en temps réel, pense sécurité

BÉNÉFICES :
✓ Détection bugs immédiate
✓ Knowledge sharing
✓ Moins de vulnérabilités
✓ Code quality supérieure

EXEMPLE SESSION :

Session : Implémenter password reset sécurisé
Duration : 2 heures
Driver : Developer A
Navigator : Developer B (focus sécurité)

Navigator checklist :
□ Token généré avec crypto.randomBytes (pas Math.random)
□ Token hashé avant stockage DB
□ Expiration 1 heure
□ One-time use (marqué used après utilisation)
□ Rate limiting forgot-password endpoint
□ Email notification user
□ Validation nouveau password (strength)
📦 Livrables Phase S4
✅ Code respectant standards sécurité
✅ Code review checklist utilisée
✅ SAST (Static Analysis) passé
✅ Dependency scan sans critical
✅ Secrets pas commités dans Git
✅ Tests de sécurité unitaires

PHASE S5 : TESTS DE SÉCURITÉ (Security Testing)
🎯 Objectif
Identifier et corriger les vulnérabilités avant la mise en production.
📋 Activités pour AdsFacile
S5.1 Types de Tests Sécurité
PYRAMIDE TESTS SÉCURITÉ

         ┌─────────────────┐
         │ Penetration     │ ← Manuel, Expert, Coûteux
         │   Testing       │   1-2 fois/an
         │   (Pentest)     │
         ├─────────────────┤
         │   DAST          │ ← Automatisé, Tests boîte noire
         │ (Dynamic)       │   Hebdomadaire/Mensuel
         ├─────────────────┤
         │   SAST          │ ← Automatisé, Tests boîte blanche
         │  (Static)       │   Chaque commit/PR
         ├─────────────────┤
         │  Unit Tests     │ ← Développeurs, Continu
         │  (Security)     │   Chaque commit
         └─────────────────┘

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. UNIT TESTS SÉCURITÉ
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Tests écrits par développeurs pour logique sécurité

// tests/security/auth.test.js

describe('Authentication Security', () => {
  
  test('should reject weak passwords', async () => {
    const weakPasswords = [
      'test',
      '12345678',
      'password',
      'Test123', // Pas de minuscule
    ];
    
    for (const password of weakPasswords) {
      const response = await request(app)
        .post('/api/auth/signup')
        .send({
          email: 'test@example.com',
          password: password,
          accept_terms: true,
        });
      
      expect(response.status).toBe(400);
      expect(response.body.errors).toContainEqual(
        expect.objectContaining({
          msg: expect.stringContaining('Password too weak')
        })
      );
    }
  });
  
  test('should rate limit login attempts', async () => {
    const attempts = [];
    
    // 6 tentatives rapides
    for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) {
      attempts.push(
        request(app)
          .post('/api/auth/login')
          .send({
            identifier: 'test@example.com',
            password: 'wrongpassword'
          })
      );
    }
    
    const responses = await Promise.all(attempts);
    const lastResponse = responses[5];
    
    expect(lastResponse.status).toBe(429);
    expect(lastResponse.body.error).toContain('Too many attempts');
  });
  
  test('should not leak user existence in login', async () => {
    // Tentative avec email qui n'existe pas
    const response1 = await request(app)
      .post('/api/auth/login')
      .send({
        identifier: 'nonexistent@example.com',
        password: 'anypassword'
      });
    
    // Tentative avec email existant mais mauvais password
    const response2 = await request(app)
      .post('/api/auth/login')
      .send({
        identifier: 'existing@example.com',
        password: 'wrongpassword'
      });
    
    // Les deux devraient retourner même message
    expect(response1.status).toBe(401);
    expect(response2.status).toBe(401);
    expect(response1.body.error).toBe(response2.body.error);
    // Pas "Email not found" vs "Wrong password"
  });
  
  test('should hash passwords with bcrypt', async () => {
    const password = 'TestPassword123!';
    
    const response = await request(app)
      .post('/api/auth/signup')
      .send({
        email: 'hash-test@example.com',
        password: password,
        accept_terms: true,
      });
    
    expect(response.status).toBe(201);
    
    // Vérifier en DB
    const { data: user } = await supabase
      .from('users')
      .select('password_hash')
      .eq('email', 'hash-test@example.com')
      .single();
    
    // Hash ne doit PAS être le password en clair
    expect(user.password_hash).not.toBe(password);
    
    // Hash doit commencer par $2a$ ou $2b$ (bcrypt)
    expect(user.password_hash).toMatch(/^\$2[ab]\$/);
    
    // Vérifier bcrypt compare fonctionne
    const match = await bcrypt.compare(password, user.password_hash);
    expect(match).toBe(true);
  });
  
});

describe('Authorization Security', () => {
  
  test('should prevent IDOR - user A cannot access user B data', async () => {
    // Créer 2 users
    const userA = await createTestUser('userA@test.com');
    const userB = await createTestUser('userB@test.com');
    
    // User B crée une campagne
    const campaign = await createTestCampaign(userB.id);
    
    // User A essaie d'accéder campagne de User B
    const response = await request(app)
      .get(`/api/campaigns/${campaign.id}`)
      .set('Authorization', `Bearer ${userA.token}`);
    
    // Doit retourner 404, pas 403 (pas leak info)
    expect(response.status).toBe(404);
    expect(response.body.error).toBe('Campaign not found');
  });
  
  test('should require authentication for protected endpoints', async () => {
    const protectedEndpoints = [
      { method: 'get', path: '/api/campaigns' },
      { method: 'post', path: '/api/campaigns' },
      { method: 'get', path: '/api/analytics/overview' },
      { method: 'post', path: '/api/ai/generate-text' },
    ];
    
    for (const endpoint of protectedEndpoints) {
      const response = await request(app)[endpoint.method](endpoint.path);
      
      expect(response.status).toBe(401);
      expect(response.body.error).toContain('Unauthorized');
    }
  });
  
});

describe('Input Validation Security', () => {
  
  test('should reject SQL injection attempts', async () => {
    const sqlInjectionPayloads = [
      "' OR '1'='1",
      "'; DROP TABLE users; --",
      "admin'--",
      "1' UNION SELECT * FROM users--",
    ];
    
    const token = await getTestUserToken();
    
    for (const payload of sqlInjectionPayloads) {
      const response = await request(app)
        .get('/api/campaigns')
        .query({ search: payload })
        .set('Authorization', `Bearer ${token}`);
      
      // Ne doit pas crash (500) et ne doit pas exécuter SQL malveillant
      expect(response.status).not.toBe(500);
      // Résultat doit être vide ou erreur validation
      expect(response.body).toBeDefined();
    }
  });
  
  test('should sanitize XSS attempts', async () => {
    const xssPayloads = [
      '<script>alert("XSS")</script>',
      '<img src=x onerror=alert("XSS")>',
      'javascript:alert("XSS")',
      '<iframe src="evil.com"></iframe>',
    ];
    
    const token = await getTestUserToken();
    
    for (const payload of xssPayloads) {
      const response = await request(app)
        .post('/api/campaigns')
        .set('Authorization', `Bearer ${token}`)
        .send({
          name: payload,
          objective: 'CONVERSIONS',
          budget_daily: 5000,
        });
      
      if (response.status === 201) {
        // Si accepté, vérifier que stocké escaped
        const campaign = response.body.data;
        expect(campaign.name).not.toContain('<script>');
        expect(campaign.name).not.toContain('onerror=');
      } else {
        // Ou rejeté par validation
        expect(response.status).toBe(400);
      }
    }
  });
  
});

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. SAST (Static Application Security Testing)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Analyse statique du code source pour détecter vulnérabilités

OUTILS :

A) GitHub CodeQL (Gratuit pour repos publics)

.github/workflows/codeql.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
name: "CodeQL"

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 0 * * 1' # Lundi minuit

jobs:
  analyze:
    name: Analyze
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    
    strategy:
      matrix:
        language: [ 'javascript' ]
    
    steps:
    - name: Checkout repository
      uses: actions/checkout@v3
    
    - name: Initialize CodeQL
      uses: github/codeql-action/init@v2
      with:
        languages: ${{ matrix.language }}
        queries: security-and-quality
    
    - name: Autobuild
      uses: github/codeql-action/autobuild@v2
    
    - name: Perform CodeQL Analysis
      uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@v2

B) SonarQube / SonarCloud

sonar-project.properties
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
sonar.projectKey=adsfacile
sonar.organization=your-org
sonar.sources=src
sonar.tests=tests
sonar.javascript.lcov.reportPaths=coverage/lcov.info
sonar.exclusions=**/node_modules/**

# Security hotspots rules
sonar.security.hotspots.enable=true

.github/workflows/sonarcloud.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
name: SonarCloud
on:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  sonarcloud:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
      
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci
      
      - name: Run tests with coverage
        run: npm run test:coverage
      
      - name: SonarCloud Scan
        uses: SonarSource/sonarcloud-github-action@master
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          SONAR_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SONAR_TOKEN }}

C) ESLint Security Plugin

.eslintrc.js
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
module.exports = {
  extends: [
    'eslint:recommended',
    'plugin:security/recommended',
  ],
  plugins: ['security'],
  rules: {
    'security/detect-object-injection': 'warn',
    'security/detect-non-literal-regexp': 'warn',
    'security/detect-unsafe-regex': 'error',
    'security/detect-buffer-noassert': 'error',
    'security/detect-child-process': 'warn',
    'security/detect-disable-mustache-escape': 'error',
    'security/detect-eval-with-expression': 'error',
    'security/detect-no-csrf-before-method-override': 'error',
    'security/detect-non-literal-fs-filename': 'warn',
    'security/detect-non-literal-require': 'warn',
    'security/detect-possible-timing-attacks': 'warn',
    'security/detect-pseudoRandomBytes': 'error',
  }
};

VULNÉRABILITÉS DÉTECTÉES PAR SAST :
✓ Hardcoded secrets
✓ SQL injection (si string concatenation détecté)
✓ Command injection
✓ Path traversal
✓ Unsafe regex (ReDoS)
✓ Weak cryptography
✓ Insecure randomness
✓ XSS (si dangerouslySetInnerHTML)
✓ eval() usage
✓ Prototype pollution

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. DAST (Dynamic Application Security Testing)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Tests boîte noire : Scanner l'application en running

OUTILS :

A) OWASP ZAP (Gratuit, Open Source)

Installation :
docker pull owasp/zap2docker-stable

Scan baseline (rapide) :
docker run -t owasp/zap2docker-stable \
  zap-baseline.py \
  -t https://staging.adsfacile.cm \
  -r zap-report.html

Scan complet (long, thorougher) :
docker run -t owasp/zap2docker-stable \
  zap-full-scan.py \
  -t https://staging.adsfacile.cm \
  -r zap-full-report.html

CI/CD Integration :
.github/workflows/zap-scan.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
name: OWASP ZAP Scan
on:
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 2 * * 1' # Lundi 2am (weekly)
  workflow_dispatch: # Manuel trigger

jobs:
  zap_scan:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: ZAP Scan
        uses: zaproxy/action-baseline@v0.7.0
        with:
          target: 'https://staging.adsfacile.cm'
          rules_file_name: '.zap/rules.tsv'
          cmd_options: '-a'
      
      - name: Upload ZAP Report
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
        with:
          name: zap-report
          path: report_html.html

B) Burp Suite (Commercial, très puissant)

Usage manuel :
1. Configurer proxy Burp (127.0.0.1:8080)
2. Naviguer app via proxy
3. Spider/Crawler automatique
4. Scanner actif
5. Analyser résultats

VULNÉRABILITÉS DÉTECTÉES PAR DAST :
✓ XSS (reflected, stored)
✓ SQL injection
✓ CSRF
✓ Broken authentication
✓ Sensitive data exposure
✓ XML External Entities (XXE)
✓ Broken access control
✓ Security misconfiguration
✓ Insecure deserialization
✓ Using components with known vulnerabilities

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
4. DEPENDENCY SCANNING
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Scanner vulnérabilités dans dépendances npm

A) npm audit (Intégré)

npm audit
npm audit fix # Auto-fix si possible
npm audit fix --force # Force majeure versions (breaking changes)

CI/CD :
.github/workflows/security.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
- name: Security Audit
  run: npm audit --audit-level=high
  # Échoue si vulnérabilités high/critical

B) Snyk (Gratuit pour open source)

Installation :
npm install -g snyk
snyk auth

Scan :
snyk test # Test vulnérabilités
snyk monitor # Monitoring continu
snyk wizard # Fix interactif

CI/CD :
- name: Run Snyk
  uses: snyk/actions/node@master
  env:
    SNYK_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SNYK_TOKEN }}

C) GitHub Dependabot (Gratuit)

.github/dependabot.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
version: 2
updates:
  - package-ecosystem: "npm"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "daily"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10
    
    # Auto-merge minor/patch (si tests passent)
    reviewers:
      - "security-team"
    
    # Ignore certaines dépendances
    ignore:
      - dependency-name: "some-package"
        versions: ["4.x"]

Dependabot crée automatiquement PRs pour updates sécurité

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
5. PENETRATION TESTING (Pentest)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Test manuel par expert sécurité (ethical hacker)

QUAND :
- Avant lancement initial production
- Annuellement
- Après changement architectural majeur
- Si stockage données sensibles (paiements, santé)

SCOPE PENTEST ADSFACILE :
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ IN SCOPE (À tester)                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • https://app.adsfacile.cm (frontend)                   │
│ • https://api.adsfacile.cm (backend)                    │
│ • Authentication flows (signup, login, reset)           │
│ • Authorization (IDOR, privilege escalation)            │
│ • API endpoints                                         │
│ • Payment flow (MeSomb integration)                     │
│ • Admin panel (si existe)                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ OUT OF SCOPE (Ne PAS tester)                            │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Social engineering (pas phishing équipe)              │
│ • Physical access (pas entrer dans bureaux)             │
│ • DoS attacks (pas crasher prod)                        │
│ • Services tiers (Facebook, OpenAI, Supabase)           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

MÉTHODOLOGIE :
1. Reconnaissance (information gathering)
2. Scanning (découvrir vulnérabilités potentielles)
3. Exploitation (confirmer vulnérabilités)
4. Post-exploitation (évaluer impact)
5. Reporting (rapport détaillé avec PoCs)

DELIVERABLES PENTEST :
✓ Executive summary (pour management)
✓ Technical report détaillé (pour devs)
✓ Proof of Concepts (PoCs) reproductibles
✓ Remediation roadmap (priorités fix)
✓ Retest après fixes

COÛT :
- Pentest basique : ~500,000 - 1,500,000 FCFA
- Pentest complet : 2,000,000 - 5,000,000 FCFA
- Retest : ~30% du coût initial

ALTERNATIVES LOW-COST :
- Bug Bounty program (HackerOne, Bugcrowd)
  - Payez uniquement bugs trouvés
  - Crowd-sourced security
- University partnerships
  - Étudiants cybersécurité testent gratuitement
S5.2 Security Testing Automation
PIPELINE CI/CD SÉCURITÉ COMPLET

.github/workflows/security-pipeline.yml
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

name: Security Pipeline

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]
  schedule:
    - cron: '0 2 * * 1' # Weekly Monday 2am

jobs:
  
  # JOB 1 : SAST (Code Analysis)
  sast:
    name: Static Analysis
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci
      
      - name: ESLint Security
        run: npm run lint
      
      - name: CodeQL Analysis
        uses: github/codeql-action/analyze@v2
      
      - name: SonarCloud Scan
        uses: SonarSource/sonarcloud-github-action@master
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          SONAR_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SONAR_TOKEN }}
  
  # JOB 2 : Dependency Scan
  dependencies:
    name: Dependency Security
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: npm audit
        run: npm audit --audit-level=high
      
      - name: Snyk Test
        uses: snyk/actions/node@master
        env:
          SNYK_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SNYK_TOKEN }}
        with:
          command: test
          args: --severity-threshold=high
  
  # JOB 3 : Secrets Scan
  secrets:
    name: Secrets Detection
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0 # Full history
      
      - name: TruffleHog Scan
        uses: trufflesecurity/trufflehog@main
        with:
          path: ./
          base: ${{ github.event.repository.default_branch }}
          head: HEAD
  
  # JOB 4 : Security Unit Tests
  security-tests:
    name: Security Unit Tests
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci
      
      - name: Run Security Tests
        run: npm run test:security
      
      - name: Upload Coverage
        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v3
        with:
          files: ./coverage/lcov.info
          flags: security
  
  # JOB 5 : DAST (si staging disponible)
  dast:
    name: Dynamic Analysis
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/develop'
    steps:
      - name: ZAP Baseline Scan
        uses: zaproxy/action-baseline@v0.7.0
        with:
          target: 'https://staging.adsfacile.cm'
          rules_file_name: '.zap/rules.tsv'
      
      - name: Upload ZAP Results
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v3
        if: always()
        with:
          name: zap-report
          path: report_html.html
  
  # JOB 6 : Container Scan (si Docker)
  container:
    name: Container Security
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Build Docker Image
        run: docker build -t adsfacile:${{ github.sha }} .
      
      - name: Trivy Scan
        uses: aquasecurity/trivy-action@master
        with:
          image-ref: adsfacile:${{ github.sha }}
          format: 'sarif'
          output: 'trivy-results.sarif'
      
      - name: Upload Trivy Results
        uses: github/codeql-action/upload-sarif@v2
        with:
          sarif_file: 'trivy-results.sarif'
  
  # JOB 7 : Security Report
  report:
    name: Security Report
    needs: [sast, dependencies, secrets, security-tests]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: always()
    steps:
      - name: Download Artifacts
        uses: actions/download-artifact@v3
      
      - name: Generate Summary
        run: |
          echo "## Security Scan Summary" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
          echo "✅ SAST: Passed" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
          echo "✅ Dependencies: Passed" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
          echo "✅ Secrets: No leaks detected" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
          echo "✅ Security Tests: All passed" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
      
      - name: Slack Notification
        if: failure()
        uses: slackapi/slack-github-action@v1
        with:
          payload: |
            {
              "text": "🚨 Security scan failed on ${{ github.repository }}",
              "blocks": [
                {
                  "type": "section",
                  "text": {
                    "type": "mrkdwn",
                    "text": "*Security Alert*\nFailed: ${{ github.workflow }}\nBranch: ${{ github.ref }}"
                  }
                }
              ]
            }
        env:
          SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL: ${{ secrets.SLACK_WEBHOOK }}

PHASE S6 : DÉPLOIEMENT SÉCURISÉ (Secure Deployment)
🎯 Objectif
Déployer en production de manière sécurisée avec hardening et configuration optimale.
📋 Activités pour AdsFacile
S6.1 Infrastructure Hardening
HARDENING SERVEUR & SERVICES

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. SERVER HARDENING (Railway/Render)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Bien que Railway/Render gèrent l'infra, vérifier :

✅ HTTPS/TLS Configuration :
  • TLS 1.3 uniquement (désactiver 1.0, 1.1, 1.2 si possible)
  • Strong cipher suites only
  • Perfect Forward Secrecy (PFS)
  • HSTS preload

✅ Environment Variables :
  • Tous secrets dans Railway Secrets (pas .env commité)
  • Rotation trimestrielle
  • Accès limité (RBAC si équipe)

✅ Logging :
  • Logs structurés (JSON)
  • Exportés vers Papertrail/CloudWatch
  • Retention policy (90 jours)

✅ Monitoring :
  • Uptime Robot (external)
  • Railway metrics (internal)
  • Sentry (errors)
  • Alerting configuré

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. APPLICATION HARDENING
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// server.js - Production config

const helmet = require('helmet');
const rateLimit = require('express-rate-limit');
const mongoSanitize = require('express-mongo-sanitize');
const hpp = require('hpp');
const cors = require('cors');

// Security headers
app.use(helmet({
  contentSecurityPolicy: {
    directives: {
      defaultSrc: ["'self'"],
      scriptSrc: ["'self'"],
      styleSrc: ["'self'", "'unsafe-inline'"],
      imgSrc: ["'self'", "data:", "https:"],
      connectSrc: ["'self'", "https://api.adsfacile.cm"],
      fontSrc: ["'self'"],
      objectSrc: ["'none'"],
      mediaSrc: ["'self'"],
      frameSrc: ["'none'"],
    },
  },
  hsts: {
    maxAge: 31536000,
    includeSubDomains: true,
    preload: true,
  },
  frameguard: {
    action: 'deny', // X-Frame-Options: DENY
  },
  noSniff: true, // X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff
  xssFilter: true, // X-XSS-Protection: 1; mode=block
  referrerPolicy: {
    policy: 'strict-origin-when-cross-origin',
  },
}));

// Hide Express
app.disable('x-powered-by');

// CORS strict
app.use(cors({
  origin: process.env.FRONTEND_URL,
  credentials: true,
  methods: ['GET', 'POST', 'PUT', 'DELETE'],
  allowedHeaders: ['Content-Type', 'Authorization'],
}));

// Rate limiting global
const limiter = rateLimit({
  windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000, // 15 minutes
  max: 100, // 100 requests per 15 min
  standardHeaders: true,
  legacyHeaders: false,
  message: 'Trop de requêtes, réessayez plus tard',
});
app.use('/api/', limiter);

// Rate limiting login spécifique
const loginLimiter = rateLimit({
  windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000,
  max: 5,
  skipSuccessfulRequests: true,
});
app.use('/api/auth/login', loginLimiter);

// Sanitize data (NoSQL injection)
app.use(mongoSanitize());

// Prevent HTTP Parameter Pollution
app.use(hpp());

// Body parser avec limite taille
app.use(express.json({ limit: '10mb' }));
app.use(express.urlencoded({ extended: true, limit: '10mb' }));

// Request ID (tracing)
app.use((req, res, next) => {
  req.id = crypto.randomUUID();
  res.setHeader('X-Request-ID', req.id);
  next();
});

// Logging requests
app.use((req, res, next) => {
  logger.info('Incoming request', {
    method: req.method,
    url: req.url,
    ip: req.ip,
    userAgent: req.get('user-agent'),
    requestId: req.id,
  });
  next();
});

// Security headers custom
app.use((req, res, next) => {
  res.setHeader('X-Content-Type-Options', 'nosniff');
  res.setHeader('X-Frame-Options', 'DENY');
  res.setHeader('X-XSS-Protection', '1; mode=block');
  res.setHeader('Referrer-Policy', 'strict-origin-when-cross-origin');
  res.setHeader('Permissions-Policy', 'geolocation=(), microphone=(), camera=()');
  next();
});

// Force HTTPS
if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') {
  app.use((req, res, next) => {
    if (req.header('x-forwarded-proto') !== 'https') {
      return res.redirect(`https://${req.header('host')}${req.url}`);
    }
    next();
  });
}

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. DATABASE HARDENING (Supabase)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

✅ Connection Security :
  • SSL/TLS obligatoire
  • Connection pooling configuré (max 10 connections)
  • Credentials dans secrets vault

✅ Access Control :
  • Database user avec least privilege
  • Pas de permissions DROP, CREATE
  • Row Level Security (RLS) si possible

-- Enable RLS
ALTER TABLE users ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE campaigns ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

-- Policy : Users can only see their own data
CREATE POLICY users_policy ON users
  FOR ALL
  USING (id = current_user_id());

CREATE POLICY campaigns_policy ON campaigns
  FOR ALL
  USING (user_id = current_user_id());

✅ Backups :
  • Automated daily backups
  • Encrypted backups
  • Offsite storage (different region)
  • Tested monthly (restore test)
  • Retention : 30 jours

✅ Monitoring :
  • Slow query log (> 1000ms)
  • Connection count
  • Database size
  • Disk usage

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
4. SECRETS MANAGEMENT
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Railway Secrets (Production) :

Secrets à configurer :
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Variable                    │ Description               │
├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
│ NODE_ENV                    │ production                │
│ PORT                        │ 5000                      │
│ FRONTEND_URL                │ https://app.adsfacile.cm  │
│ JWT_SECRET                  │ [256-bit random]          │
│ SUPABASE_URL                │ https://xxx.supabase.co   │
│ SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY        │ [secret key]              │
│ OPENAI_API_KEY              │ sk-...                    │
│ FACEBOOK_APP_ID             │ [app id]                  │
│ FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET         │ [app secret]              │
│ MESOMB_API_KEY              │ [api key]                 │
│ BREVO_API_KEY               │ [api key]                 │
│ ENCRYPTION_KEY              │ [256-bit hex]             │
│ SENTRY_DSN                  │ https://...               │
└─────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘

Comment générer secrets forts :

# JWT_SECRET (256 bits)
node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('hex'))"

# ENCRYPTION_KEY (256 bits)
node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('hex'))"

Rotation Policy :
- JWT_SECRET : Tous les 90 jours
- API Keys externes : Quand provider suggère
- ENCRYPTION_KEY : Annuellement (require data re-encryption)

Accès secrets :
- Uniquement via environment variables
- JAMAIS loggés
- JAMAIS exposés dans API responses
- Accès limité (RBAC si équipe)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
5. MONITORING & ALERTING PRODUCTION
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Sentry Configuration :

// server.js
const Sentry = require('@sentry/node');
const { ProfilingIntegration } = require('@sentry/profiling-node');

Sentry.init({
  dsn: process.env.SENTRY_DSN,
  environment: process.env.NODE_ENV,
  release: process.env.GIT_COMMIT_SHA,
  
  // Performance Monitoring
  tracesSampleRate: 1.0,
  
  // Profiling
  profilesSampleRate: 1.0,
  integrations: [
    new ProfilingIntegration(),
  ],
  
  // Filter sensitive data
  beforeSend(event, hint) {
    // Remove passwords from event
    if (event.request) {
      delete event.request.data?.password;
      delete event.request.data?.confirmPassword;
    }
    
    // Remove tokens
    if (event.request?.headers?.authorization) {
      event.request.headers.authorization = '[FILTERED]';
    }
    
    return event;
  },
});

// Request handler (doit être premier middleware)
app.use(Sentry.Handlers.requestHandler());

// Tracing handler
app.use(Sentry.Handlers.tracingHandler());

// ... app routes ...

// Error handler (doit être dernier middleware)
app.use(Sentry.Handlers.errorHandler());

Uptime Robot :
  • Monitor : https://api.adsfacile.cm/health
  • Interval : 5 minutes
  • Alert : Email + SMS si down
  • Locations : Multiple (US, EU, Asia)

PostHog Analytics :
  • Track business events
  • User behavior
  • Conversion funnels
  • A/B testing (futur)

Custom Metrics Dashboard :
  • Active users (DAU, MAU)
  • Response times (p50, p95, p99)
  • Error rate
  • Payment success rate
  • AI generation usage
  • Campaign creation rate

Alerting Rules :
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Metric                  │ Threshold  │ Action          │
├─────────────────────────┼────────────┼─────────────────┤
│ Error rate              │ > 5%       │ Slack + PagerDuty│
│ Response time p95       │ > 2s       │ Slack           │
│ Uptime                  │ < 99.5%    │ SMS + Email     │
│ Database connections    │ > 80%      │ Slack           │
│ Disk usage              │ > 85%      │ Email           │
│ Payment failure rate    │ > 10%      │ Slack critical  │
│ Sentry new error type   │ Any        │ Slack           │
└─────────────────────────┴────────────┴─────────────────┘

S6.3 Incident Response Plan
PLAN DE RÉPONSE AUX INCIDENTS SÉCURITÉ

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
DÉFINITION INCIDENT SÉCURITÉ
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Incident = Événement qui :
- Compromet confidentialité, intégrité ou disponibilité des données
- Viole politique de sécurité
- Potentiel de causer des dommages

Exemples :
- Data breach (fuite données utilisateurs)
- Compte admin compromis
- Injection SQL exploitée
- DDoS attack
- Malware détecté
- Accès non autorisé détecté

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
SÉVÉRITÉ INCIDENTS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

CRITICAL (P0) :
- Data breach avec données sensibles (passwords, tokens)
- Production completely down
- Accès admin compromis
- Ransomware
→ Response Time : < 15 minutes
→ All hands on deck

HIGH (P1) :
- Vulnérabilité critique exploitée
- Unauthorized access (tentatives multiples)
- Payment system compromis
→ Response Time : < 1 heure
→ Security team + on-call

MEDIUM (P2) :
- Vulnérabilité high détectée (pas encore exploitée)
- DDoS attack mineur
- Suspicious activity patterns
→ Response Time : < 4 heures
→ On-call developer

LOW (P3) :
- Vulnérabilité medium/low
- Configuration issue
→ Response Time : < 24 heures
→ Next sprint

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
INCIDENT RESPONSE LIFECYCLE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PHASE 1 : DÉTECTION & IDENTIFICATION (0-15 min)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Sources détection :
- Sentry alert (error spike)
- Uptime Robot (downtime)
- User report
- Monitoring anomaly
- Security scan finding

Actions :
1. Confirmer incident (pas faux positif)
2. Déterminer sévérité (P0/P1/P2/P3)
3. Créer incident ticket (GitHub Issue)
4. Activer incident response team

Template incident ticket :
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Title : [SECURITY INCIDENT P0] Data Breach Suspected

**Detected** : 2026-02-11 14:30 UTC
**Reporter** : @developer-name
**Severity** : P0 - CRITICAL

**Description**
Unusual database queries detected accessing all user emails.
Possible SQL injection exploitation.

**Affected Systems**
- API server (api.adsfacile.cm)
- PostgreSQL database (Supabase)

**Affected Data**
- Potentially : All user emails (5000 users)
- Potentially : User metadata

**Initial Actions Taken**
- [ ] Isolated affected server
- [ ] Disabled compromised endpoint
- [ ] Created database snapshot

**Incident Commander** : @tech-lead
**Responders** : @dev1, @dev2
**Communications** : @support-lead
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PHASE 2 : CONTAINMENT (15 min - 2h)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objectif : Limiter propagation, empêcher aggravation

Actions SHORT-TERM containment :
- Isoler systèmes affectés (disable endpoint, firewall rules)
- Changer tous credentials exposés
- Revoke JWT tokens (invalidate sessions)
- Enable enhanced logging
- Preserve evidence (logs, DB snapshots)

Actions LONG-TERM containment :
- Patch vulnerability
- Deploy fix to production
- Enhanced monitoring
- Restore from backup si nécessaire

PHASE 3 : INVESTIGATION (Parallèle à containment)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objectif : Root cause analysis

Questions clés :
- Comment l'attaquant a-t-il obtenu accès ?
- Quand l'incident a-t-il commencé ?
- Quelles données ont été compromises ?
- Combien d'users affectés ?
- L'attaquant a-t-il encore accès ?
- Autres systèmes compromis ?

Evidence collection :
- Server logs
- Database logs
- Sentry events
- Network traffic logs
- Git history (code changes récents)
- User reports

Tools :
- grep, awk pour parser logs
- SQL queries pour analyser DB
- Git blame pour code changes
- Sentry breadcrumbs pour tracer requests

PHASE 4 : ÉRADICATION (2-8h)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objectif : Éliminer cause racine

Actions :
- Supprimer malware si applicable
- Fermer backdoors
- Patch vulnérabilité
- Renforcer sécurité (hardening)
- Révoquer accès compromis
- Reset credentials

Validation :
- Vulnerability scan confirme fix
- Penetration test ciblé
- Code review patch
- No persistent access remaining

PHASE 5 : RECOVERY (8-24h)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objectif : Restaurer opérations normales

Actions :
- Restore clean data (from backup si nécessaire)
- Redéployer services
- Monitoring intensif
- Gradual rollout (canary deployment)
- Vérifier intégrité données

Validation :
- All systems operational
- No anomalies detected
- Users can access normally
- Business metrics normal

PHASE 6 : POST-INCIDENT (24-72h après résolution)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objectif : Apprendre et améliorer

1. POST-MORTEM Meeting (dans les 48h)
   Participants : Incident team + stakeholders
   Durée : 1-2 heures
   
   Agenda :
   • Timeline incident (heure par heure)
   • Root cause analysis
   • What went well ?
   • What went wrong ?
   • Lessons learned
   • Action items (avec owners & deadlines)

2. POST-MORTEM Document (template)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Post-Mortem : Data Breach via SQL Injection

## Executive Summary
[2-3 phrases pour management]

## Timeline
- 14:00 : Suspicious queries detected
- 14:05 : Incident declared (P0)
- 14:15 : Endpoint disabled
- 14:30 : Root cause identified
- 15:00 : Patch deployed
- 16:00 : Incident resolved
- Duration : 2 hours

## Impact
- Users affected : 5,000 (100%)
- Data exposed : Email addresses
- Data NOT exposed : Passwords (hashed), payment info
- Downtime : 45 minutes (endpoint disabled)
- Revenue loss : ~50,000 FCFA (estimated)

## Root Cause
SQL injection vulnerability in `/api/campaigns` search endpoint.
String concatenation instead of parameterized query.

Code :
```javascript
// VULNERABLE CODE
const query = `SELECT * FROM campaigns WHERE name LIKE '%${searchTerm}%'`;
```

Attack vector :
Attacker sent : `searchTerm='; SELECT * FROM users--`

## Detection
- Sentry alert : High error rate
- Manual review logs
- Time to detect : 5 minutes (good)

## Response
[Détails actions prises]

## What Went Well
✓ Quick detection (5 min)
✓ Clear incident process followed
✓ Good communication
✓ Backup available

## What Went Wrong
✗ Vulnerability existed (code review missed)
✗ No SAST configured (would have caught)
✗ Delayed notification users (2h)

## Lessons Learned
1. NEVER use string concatenation for SQL
2. SAST mandatory before merge
3. Security code review checklist
4. Faster user communication

## Action Items
| Action | Owner | Deadline | Status |
|--------|-------|----------|--------|
| Implement SAST (CodeQL) | @dev | 2026-02-15 | ☐ |
| Security training team | @techlead | 2026-02-20 | ☐ |
| Code review checklist | @dev | 2026-02-13 | ☐ |
| Penetration test | @manager | 2026-03-01 | ☐ |
| User communication plan | @support | 2026-02-14 | ☐ |

## Related Documents
- Incident ticket : #123
- Code fix PR : #456
- User notification email : [link]
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

3. Communication Users (RGPD : < 72h si data breach)

Email template :
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Objet : Incident de sécurité - Vos données

Cher utilisateur,

Nous vous informons qu'un incident de sécurité a affecté notre 
plateforme le 11 février 2026.

**Ce qui s'est passé**
Une vulnérabilité a permis un accès non autorisé à notre base 
de données entre 14h00 et 15h00.

**Données potentiellement affectées**
- Votre adresse email
- Votre nom et prénom
- Votre ville

**Données NON affectées**

- Vos données de paiement (non stockées chez nous)

- Renforcement de nos mesures de sécurité

L'équipe AdsFacile

4. Authorities notification (si requis RGPD)

S7.1 Continuous Security
ACTIVITÉS SÉCURITÉ CONTINUES

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

  • Temps : 5 minutes

S7.2 Patch Management
GESTION DES PATCHES & UPDATES

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
POLICY PATCHING
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

CRITICAL Security Patches :
- Timeframe : < 24 heures
- Process : Hotfix immédiat
- Testing : Minimal (staging smoke test)
- Approval : Tech lead
- Example : CVE with CVSS score 9-10

HIGH Security Patches :
- Timeframe : < 7 jours
- Process : Regular release cycle
- Testing : Full test suite
- Approval : Code review
- Example : CVE with CVSS score 7-8.9

MEDIUM/LOW Security Patches :
- Timeframe : < 30 jours
- Process : Next sprint
- Testing : Full suite + regression
- Approval : Normal PR process
- Example : CVE with CVSS score < 7

REGULAR Updates (non-security) :
- Timeframe : Quarterly
- Process : Planned upgrade
- Testing : Extensive
- Example : React 18.2 → 18.3

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PATCH WORKFLOW
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

1. DETECTION
   Sources :
   • GitHub Dependabot alert
   • npm audit
   • Snyk scan
   • CVE databases monitoring
   • Vendor security advisories

2. TRIAGE
   Questions :
   • Severity ? (Critical/High/Medium/Low)
   • Affected component used in our app ?
   • Exploit available publicly ?
   • Users data at risk ?
   
   Decision :
   • If Critical + Exploitable → EMERGENCY PATCH
   • Else → Regular patching cycle

3. TESTING
   Local :
   • Update dependency
   • Run test suite
   • Manual testing critical paths
   
   Staging :
   • Deploy to staging
   • Smoke tests
   • Monitor 24h

4. DEPLOYMENT
   Production :
   • Deploy during low-traffic (3am local time)
   • Monitor closely 1h post-deploy
   • Rollback plan ready

5. VERIFICATION
   • Dependency scan confirms fix
   • No regressions detected
   • Close security ticket

6. DOCUMENTATION
   • Update CHANGELOG
   • Internal security log
   • If user-impacting : Release notes

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
AUTOMATION PATCHING
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

GitHub Dependabot Config :

.github/dependabot.yml
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
version: 2
updates:
  - package-ecosystem: "npm"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "daily"
      time: "03:00"
      timezone: "Africa/Douala"
    
    # Auto-merge rules
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10
    
    # Security updates only (auto-merge)
    labels:
      - "security"
      - "dependencies"
    
    # Grouping
    groups:
      security-patches:
        patterns:
          - "*"
        update-types:
          - "patch"
    
    # Ignore major versions (manual review)
    ignore:
      - dependency-name: "*"
        update-types: ["version-update:semver-major"]

Renovate (Alternative) :

renovate.json
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
{
  "extends": ["config:base"],
  "schedule": ["before 4am on Monday"],
  "automerge": true,
  "automergeType": "pr",
  "automergeStrategy": "squash",
  "packageRules": [
    {
      "matchUpdateTypes": ["patch", "pin", "digest"],
      "matchPackagePatterns": ["*"],
      "automerge": true
    },
    {
      "matchDepTypes": ["devDependencies"],
      "matchUpdateTypes": ["minor"],
      "automerge": true
    },
    {
      "matchPackagePatterns": ["^eslint", "^prettier"],
      "groupName": "linters"
    }
  ],
  "vulnerabilityAlerts": {
    "enabled": true,
    "schedule": ["at any time"]
  }
}
S7.3 Security Metrics & KPIs
MESURE DE LA SÉCURITÉ

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
KEY PERFORMANCE INDICATORS (KPIs)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

1. MEAN TIME TO DETECT (MTTD)
   Définition : Temps moyen entre incident et détection
   Target : < 5 minutes
   Mesure : Timestamp incident - Timestamp detection
   
   Comment améliorer :
   • Better monitoring
   • Automated alerting
   • Anomaly detection AI

2. MEAN TIME TO RESPOND (MTTR)
   Définition : Temps moyen entre détection et containment
   Target : < 15 minutes (Critical), < 1h (High)
   Mesure : Timestamp containment - Timestamp detection
   
   Comment améliorer :
   • Incident response plan rehearsals
   • Automated containment scripts
   • Clear escalation paths

3. MEAN TIME TO REMEDIATE (MTTR)
   Définition : Temps moyen entre détection et résolution complète
   Target : < 4h (Critical), < 24h (High)
   Mesure : Timestamp resolution - Timestamp detection
   
   Comment améliorer :
   • Faster patching process
   • Pre-approved fixes
   • Better testing automation

4. VULNERABILITY DENSITY
   Définition : Nombre vulnérabilités / 1000 lignes code
   Target : < 0.5
   Mesure : SAST findings / (Total LoC / 1000)
   
   Comment améliorer :
   • SecurityContinuer06:29training
• Code reviews
• Secure coding standards

PATCH COMPLIANCE
Définition : % patches appliqués dans SLA
Target : > 95%
Mesure : Patches on-time / Total patches
Critical : < 24h
High : < 7 days
Medium : < 30 days
SECURITY TEST COVERAGE
Définition : % code couvert par security tests
Target : > 80%
Mesure : Security test coverage / Total coverage
Comment améliorer :
• Write more security unit tests
• SAST integration
• Automated security regression tests
FALSE POSITIVE RATE
Définition : % alerts qui sont faux positifs
Target : < 20%
Mesure : False positives / Total alerts
Comment améliorer :
• Tune SAST/DAST tools
• Custom rules
• Whitelist known safe patterns
SECURITY TRAINING COMPLETION
Définition : % team ayant complété training annuel
Target : 100%
Mesure : Team members trained / Total team

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// scripts/generate-security-report.js
// Run monthly, auto-send to team
const generateReport = async () => {
const vulnerabilities = await getVulnerabilities();
const incidents = await getIncidents();
const metrics = await calculateMetrics();
const report = formatReport({ vulnerabilities, incidents, metrics });
await sendToSlack(report);
await saveToNotion(report);
};

# 🎓 RÉSUMÉ FINAL : SDLC vs SSDLC
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              COMPARAISON SDLC CLASSIQUE vs SECURE SDLC        │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                               │
│ SDLC CLASSIQUE :                                              │
│ Planification → Analyse → Design → Dev → Tests → Deploy      │
│                                                     ↑          │
│                           Sécurité ajoutée ici (TROP TARD)   │
│                                                               │
│ COÛT FIX BUG SÉCURITÉ :                                       │
│ • En design     : 1x   (facile)                               │
│ • En dev        : 10x  (plus difficile)                       │
│ • En production : 100x (très coûteux)                         │
│                                                               │
│────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────  │
│                                                               │
│ SECURE SDLC (SSDLC) :                                         │
│ Planification 🔒 → Analyse 🔒 → Design 🔒 → Dev 🔒 →         │
│ → Tests 🔒 → Deploy 🔒 → Maintenance 🔒                      │
│        ↑          ↑         ↑        ↑       ↑       ↑       │
│    Sécurité intégrée À CHAQUE PHASE                          │
│                                                               │
│ BÉNÉFICES :                                                   │
│ ✅ Vulnérabilités détectées tôt (moins coûteux)              │
│ ✅ Security by design (pas afterthought)                     │
│ ✅ Conformité légale (RGPD, etc.)                            │
│ ✅ Confiance utilisateurs                                    │
│ ✅ Réduction risques business                                │
│ ✅ Coût total ownership réduit                               │
│                                                               │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
CHECKLIST GLOBALE SSDLC POUR ADSFACILE :
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
S1 - PLANIFICATION SÉCURISÉE :
☑️ Assets critiques identifiés
☑️ Threat modeling (STRIDE) complété
☑️ Security requirements définis
☑️ Compliance identifiée (RGPD, Facebook)
S2 - ANALYSE SÉCURISÉE :
☑️ 50+ security requirements documentés
☑️ Abuse cases écrits
☑️ Security test cases préparés
S3 - DESIGN SÉCURISÉ :
☑️ Architecture sécurisée documentée
☑️ Secure design patterns appliqués
☑️ Defense in depth implémenté
☑️ Encryption strategy définie
S4 - DÉVELOPPEMENT SÉCURISÉ :
☑️ Secure coding standards respectés
☑️ Code reviews avec security checklist
☑️ Secrets management (pas de hardcoded)
☑️ Input validation partout
☑️ Output encoding systématique
S5 - TESTS SÉCURITÉ :
☑️ Security unit tests (> 80% coverage)
☑️ SAST configuré (CodeQL, SonarCloud)
☑️ DAST mensuel (OWASP ZAP)
☑️ Dependency scan quotidien
☑️ Pentest annuel (ou bug bounty)
S6 - DÉPLOIEMENT SÉCURISÉ :
☑️ Infrastructure hardening
☑️ Secrets vault (Railway secrets)
☑️ Monitoring & alerting configurés
☑️ Security headers validés
☑️ Incident response plan prêt
S7 - MAINTENANCE SÉCURISÉE :
☑️ Continuous monitoring (24/7)
☑️ Patch management process
☑️ Security metrics tracking
☑️ Regular security training
☑️ Compliance audits à jour
